# Quant experiments

Every experiment in the quants subproject, planned or done, so that nothing is run twice. Check this page before
starting anything; add a row before an experiment starts and fill it in when it ends. Results are summarized here and
written up in [README.md](README.md) or [research.md](research.md); raw logs are kept outside the repository.

## Plan

**Quality bar (decided 2026-10-23):** a Flint file counts only if it is within noise of Unsloth's UD-Q4_K_XL against
BF16 (KLD 0.120, 87.7 % same top token, perplexity +3.9 %; in practice KLD ≲ 0.125 and same top token ≳ 87.5 %).
People use Unsloth's quants, and nobody trades ~9 points of token agreement for speed, so files at the makers' Q4_1
level (78–80 %: makers-fast, S1, S2, the Q4_0 variants) are no longer release candidates or targets. Speed claims compare
against UD-Q4_K_XL on the same build and cards. Engine and kernel work still counts wherever it is measured, because it
speeds up every file.

**Now (2026-11-02):** Qwen3.8-Flash-Next leads, Swift-1.5 GSQ-RCO Q2_0 first, with IQ3_XXS as the quality reference: where the
n-gram table lives (Q214, Q435), the fit probe (Q213), then the baseline and agent check (Q215). Strata's techniques are the
port list (Q436–Q471, [strata.md](../docs/research/notes/strata.md)), with Q215's profile setting their order. Nothing is
running: the rig is off and the quant machine is being rebuilt.

**Until then (2026-10-27, 20:40):** v2.0 is released; v3 puts Ornith-1.5-122B first (Q158, Q164, Q166, Q167, Q170, Q171)
with Flint's MTP head alongside (Q169, Q168). The rig is off for the night; from 2026-10-28 08:00 it runs Ornith's
attention share and the round-2 IQ kernels on UD-Q2_K_XL (Q166), the cheaper MTP guess and the answer store as paired
replays, then the overnight MTP heads (Q169). The 7-GPU quant machine runs overnight: Flint's MTP heads on two RTX 3090s
until ~08:00 (Q169), the Ornith quality runs on the V100s (Q170, Q171, Q164's files c and b), Flint near-Q8_0 on the
other three cards (Q168), and Q164's files b and d on the CPU. Keep this line current: say what is running, where, and
since when. 2026-10-28: Ornith-1.5-35B's files from its BF16 are measured (Q159: P-Q5 and PK-Q4-i lead); its agent
check is planned (Q311).

### Priorities (v2)

The list is ordered by expected value for the v2 release: Flint faster than Unsloth's UD-Q4_K_XL at its quality,
measured on this rig against stock llama.cpp and against UD-Q4_K_XL on the same build.

| # | Goal | Experiments | State | Next step | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Release file:** the best quality at equal speed against UD-Q4_K_XL with the same head treatment (screened head on both). On our engine the lead is K2sr (K2 + screened Q8_0 head + F16 router, 2.053 GB per token; Q126); for stock llama.cpp K2h-r16 (2.091 GB, GPTQ head). UD-Q4_K_XL with the screened head on our engine is the candidate to beat | Q41, Q126, Q127, Q125, Q106, Q112, Q113, Q129, Q42 | KLD at 512 tokens G3h 0.106 @ 2.21 GB, K2h 0.109 @ 2.14, K1h 0.112 @ 2.10 (UD 0.120); at 8k on agent text all 1.03–1.04× UD (BF16-confirmed); E2 miss_param behind UD (exploratory); reasoning lengths level; with screened heads on both, est. no per-token edge for K2h | Q41's confirmatory miss_param test (until 12:00); Q107's agent-text scores; Q112's GPTQ groups; rig: Q125 (same head treatment) and the candidates' speed; the decision run (799 tasks × K ≥ 2, E3 at 64k) needs owner time | rig; quant machine |
| 2 | **The pipelined executor:** keep the idle cards busy, with guessed tokens, race samples or other conversations | Q87, Q104, Q93, then Q20 as a one-token drafter | simulated 1.34× untrained, 2.3× at 70 % acceptance; executor being proven correct | rig timing of the executor; if it holds, train the drafter | code; rig; quant machine |
| 3 | **Agent traffic:** tool requests turn off backend sampling and, by its rule, the screened head, so on agent work v2 decodes only ~1.10× faster per token; keep tool requests on the GPU path, and measure real agent work end to end | Q55, Q66, Q67, Q131, Q132, Q120, Q121, Q119, Q65, Q68, Q118 ✓, Q122 (parked) | every pi/OpenCode request carries tools, so sampling runs on the host with the 600 KB logits copy (est. 3.2–3.5 ms per token) and the screen is off; Q118: no server gap for plain chat; agent-check suite designed (Q131) | Q55 on the rig today (1 h); Q67 in the fork (GPU sampling with a host grammar check, est. +9–11 % on tool requests, plus the screen kept); Q131: build the suite, calibrate on the quant machine, then the rig pilot | rig; code |
| 4 | **Answer time: shorter reasoning** (two thirds of Flint's output is reasoning) | Q93, Q94, Q85 | the lossy-weight room under the bar is ~1 ms per token; shorter reasoning is the largest wall-clock lever left | Q93 from Q41's logs (no GPU), Q94's reflection count; then task checks with Q41's harness | quant machine (tasks); rig later |
| 5 | **Prompt processing** | Q108, Q109, Q110, Q116, Q111, Q89, Q80 | Q108 (PR #31047), Q109 (pipeline fill) and Q110 (on-card mask) built, tests pass, logits identical; rig tarballs ready; est. combined 130–190 instead of 56–72 generated tok/s for tool-heavy agents | rig: copies × `-ub` grid, then the prefill and gpumask builds against v2 (runbook step 5); Q116 (decode mask) after that | code; rig |

| 7 | **Long context** | Q79, Q54, then Q59/Q60 | 32k fine (23.3 tok/s, 9 cards); the 64k drop is most likely llama-bench's saved state swapping the host | rig check without saved state; build the attention kernel only if attention is ≥ 30 % of a token at depth | rig |
| 8 | **Formats and the head** | Q92, Q112, Q113, Q114, Q115, Q90, Q91 ✓, Q56 ✓ | screened head exact offline (−0.153 GB per token, no quality cost); GPTQ Q5_0 head +0.002 KLD; Q6_K far gentler than Q5_0 (needs a Pascal 6-bit kernel); published GPTQ removes 0–13 % of imatrix rounding's cost, 68 % on Flint's head; F16 router saves 49 MB per token for free | Q113 F16 router into every candidate; Q112 GPTQ on three always-read groups (~3 GPU-hours); then Q114 experts, Q115 Q6_K; screened head in the fork (Q92); decide on a 6-bit kernel after the round-5 mixes | code; quant machine; rig |
| 9 | **Qwen3.6 track** | Q82, Q43 | Unsloth UD-Q4_K_XL KLD 0.013 sets its bar; MTP parked (1.04×) | rig speed of UD-Q4_K_XL on the fork | rig |
| 10 | **Owner decisions** | Q133, Q134, Q135, Q83, Q86 | memory offset est. +3–5 % decode at +10 % clock, tested exactly (KLD 0 when stable); PCIe 2.0 and power limits give nothing | the owner's OK for Q133 on GPU0 after the timed blocks (console within reach); Q134's package hold | rig, owner |

Parked: batched speculation plumbing (Q52, Q53) until Q78 lowers verify costs; Q37 (shared expert as routed experts),
superseded by Q50 and Q88; Q40 (quantization-aware recovery), since Q5_0 at ~1.1× Q4_1's cost reaches the bar without
retraining.

| 2026-10-23 | v2 work on the public repo's `v2` branch and on the fork's `pascal-p106-v2`; v1 (`main`, `pascal-p106`) stays as released | v1 released at `1a35d59` | five post-v1 commits stay on `main`; feature branches land on `pascal-p106-v2` |

| 2026-10-23 | The makers' Q4_1 and makers-fast stay out of Q41 | Q74: their INT4 is plain round-to-nearest of BF16, so KLD judges it fairly | Q41 compares UD-Q4_K_XL with our own candidates only |

## Registry

Status is **planned**, **running**, **done** or **dropped**. Quality numbers are same top token / mean KL divergence /
perplexity change against the reference named in the row, on 80 × 512 tokens of wikitext-2. Speed is real-chat tok/s on
the rig (llama.cpp `d77256999` + CUDA graphs, `-bs`, 262,144-token context) unless the row says otherwise.

Head alone right 53.8 % of its guesses (offline Q51 overall 0.538). One guess costs 5.3–7.3 ms on a P106 (window 4–8 rows), 2–3× the estimate: the limit now. Offline (Q51, coupled, α 3): R2-9 0.476 fresh / 0.538 overall on the release split, R2-gen 0.523 / 0.583 on six cards. Details, method and changelog: [drafter](../docs/drafter.md). Malformed 0.75 % (UD) against 0.46 % (G3), runaway 0.23 % each: format gate passed. **Full pool, attempt 1 (3,477 tasks): G3 passes with almost no room:** pass@1 UD-Q4_K_XL 76.5 %, G3 76.1 %; G3 − UD −0.40 points, 90 % interval −1.46 to +0.65 against the −1.5 margin; malformed 0.72 % vs 0.69 %, runaway 0.17 % each. Category gaps (simple_python −3.7, parallel_multiple −7.5, parallel +5.5) are within one category's ~3-point noise at one attempt. **With attempt 3 on the first half (K = 2 on 1,738 tasks): G3 − UD −0.33 points, 90 % interval −1.24 to +0.58**, more room; full-pool sampling noise E[p(1−p)] 0.077, the first two attempts disagree on 15.5 %. **K2h passes single-turn non-inferiority on all 3,477 tasks (attempt 1): K2h 76.68 %, UD 76.53 %, K2h − UD +0.14 points, 90 % interval −0.87 to +1.16**; malformed 15 against 25 answers, runaway 7 against 6, a second `</think>` 18 against 32. **E1 decided for G3: non-inferior on all 3,477 single-turn tasks at K = 2:** UD 76.30 %, G3 76.65 %, G3 − UD +0.35 points over 6,954 matched samples, 90 % interval −0.40 to +1.09; malformed 0.75 % each, runaway 0.22 % against 0.16 %. Noise E[p(1−p)] 0.076 / 0.073, attempts disagree on 15.1 / 14.6 %. E2 noise (UD, 2 attempts on the pilot): E[p(1−p)] 0.100, self-discordance 20 %, so the 799-task lane needs K ≈ 2. **First LiveCodeBench pair (E3, 56 problems, K = 1, 32k budget, graded offline without network): UD 85.7 %, G3 80.4 %, G3 − UD −5.4 points (90 % interval −10.4 to −0.3) on only 3 discordant problems, all hard and all UD's (exact McNemar p = 0.25):** two where G3 hit 32,768 tokens with no code while UD finished at 32,144 and 23,005, one wrong answer at 32,763. Easy and medium 100 % for both, hard 71.4 vs 60.7 %, the 2025-02+ subset 88.2 % for both; G3 averages 13.3k tokens per sample against UD's 12.9k. At a 32k budget small length differences decide pass or fail, so E3 compares files only at 65,536 tokens. Cross-GPU pairing adds no noise (K2h–UD disagree on 13.9 %, G3–UD on the same GPUs on 14.6 %). K2h's second half, then K1h, running on GPUs 5–6. Shared seeds buy no variance reduction (UD a1 vs G3 a1 disagree on 14.3 %, a1 vs a3 on 14.4 %), so pairs may cross GPU sets. **First multi-turn pair (E2, 100 pilot tasks, K = 1): inconclusive, negative point estimate:** UD 39 %, G3 32 %, G3 − UD −7.0 points, 90 % interval −15.3 to +1.3 (SE 4.3, 5.0 clustered by scenario); the gap is mostly in miss_param (14 vs 8 of 25); malformed conversations 11 vs 14. E2's noise (verdicts differ on 19 % of tasks; E[p(1−p)] 0.118) needs K ≈ 2–3 on the 799-task lane; attempt 2 on the same 100 tasks running **New scenarios (49 tasks, disjoint from the pilot): G3 − UD 0.0 points (SE 8.3); combined 149 tasks at attempt 1: −4.7 points, SE 3.97 (4.34 clustered by scenario), 90 % interval −11.8 to +2.4, still inconclusive;** the pilot's −7.0 shrinks as scenarios are added; the format gate Δ +1.3 (upper +7.0) is too wide to call. The decision still needs all 799 tasks at K ≥ 2. **At 199 tasks: −5.0 points (SE 3.5, clustered 3.8; 90 % interval −11.2 to +1.2), discordant 30 against 20, McNemar p 0.20. One category replicates: miss_param UD 24, G3 14 of 47 (discordant 12 against 2, exact p 0.013, ≈ 0.05 after Bonferroni; pilot 14 vs 8 of 25, new scenarios 10 vs 6 of 22); G3 asks for the missing value as often as UD (44.7 % against 43.5 % of missing-value turns, `bench/bfcl_flint/ask_rate.py`); the deficit comes after the clarification: pass when it asked 38 % against UD's 75 %, when it acted 23 % against 35 %, and once given the value G3 more often ends with a wrong call result (6) or a wrong backend state (5); exploratory one-sided McNemar p = 0.0065.** base 25 vs 22, long_context 20 vs 19, miss_func 4 vs 8 (G3's favour) show nothing. **Pre-registered confirmatory test (139 fresh miss_param tasks, attempt 1, all pairs complete at 10:24): not supported; the exploratory signal was a false positive.** UD 34 (24.5 %), G3 46 (33.1 %); discordant 11 against 23; one-sided exact McNemar p = 0.988. G3 − UD +8.6 points (90 % interval +1.8 to +15.4); V100 set +15.9, GPUs 0–2 +1.4. In the turn missing a value UD asked 36.9 %, G3 33.6 %; pass when asked 44 against 47 %, when acted 12 against 26 %; failure types after asking alike (state mismatch 14 each). Exploratory and confirmatory together (descriptive): UD 58, G3 60 of 186. **Final E2 at attempt 1, 458 tasks: UD 150 (32.5 %), G3 154 (33.6 %), G3 − UD +0.87 points (SE 2.31, clustered 2.49; 90 % interval −3.23 to +4.98)**, 0.23 points short of the 3-point non-inferiority margin at K = 1; by category (UD/G3): base 44/41 of 89, long_context 37/32 of 92, miss_func 11/21 of 91, miss_param 58/60 of 186; without miss_param 92/94 of 272; format gate Δ +1.31 (upper +4.32). **New scenarios alone (358 tasks, the pilot's 100 left out): G3 − UD +3.07 points (90 % interval −1.59 to +7.73), inside the 3-point margin, but the format gate fails (Δ +1.40, upper +4.81).** **E1 decided: G3 non-inferior at K = 2 (+0.35, 90 % −0.40 to +1.09); K2h non-inferior at K = 1 (+0.14, 90 % −0.87 to +1.16).** E3's second pair was dropped for the miss_param test. Held jobs for a decision run are kept. Pilot (UD, 376 tasks × 5): pass@1 75.7 %, sampled attempts disagree on 17.8 % of tasks (greedy 11 %), so the full E1 pool needs 2 attempts. Per-task verdicts, ID lists and the pre-registration: [main runs](../docs/data/2026-10-24_q41/README.md), [pilot](../docs/data/2026-10-23_q41_pilot/README.md) | Protocol: S4-L0 and S5 against UD-Q4_K_XL (Q8_0 as anchor, BF16 on E1 only), sampled the makers' way ([rec note](../docs/research/notes/rec_engine.md): T 1.0, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, min-p 0, reasoning kept in the history, ≥ 5 attempts with pass@1 averaged; the makers call greedy decoding "not validated"). E1 BFCL v4 single-turn, 3,491 tasks, margin 1.5 points; E2 BFCL v4 multi-turn, 800 tasks, 3 points; E3 LiveCodeBench v6 (454 problems), 3 points. Pass if the lower 90 % bound of the paired difference is above −margin on every lane and malformed plus runaway outputs are at most 0.5 points above UD-Q4_K_XL's. A pilot first; GPU-hours from Q70/Q71's pilot plan (~180 for two attempts per task). Needs Q70 and Q71 ([note](../docs/research/notes/task_level_quality_checks.md)) |

Allowing it doubles the down-projections (expert down 14 → 29 GB/s, shared down 25 → 50) and unfused K = 2048 products (attn_q 43 → 81) but slows the fused gate/up (52 → 34), so the one-liner must exclude fused calls. | Q49 | With the Pascal kernels, does the 2-core host now limit the token rate? On S4-L0 the wall time per token fell less than the GPU time (Q38) | done (partly) | Not within a token: the host launches all 6 cards' graphs within ~1.9 ms of a token's start, while each card computes 4–8 ms (the copies between cards are asynchronous). Both event profilers put the kernels' saving at ~11.4 ms of GPU time per token (GPU chain 37.9 → 26.6 ms over the 6 cards), but llama-bench's token time falls 7.5 ms (34.25 → 26.76), and with the kernels off the unprofiled token is shorter than the profiled chain. CUDA events change the timing on these cards: 12 events per token slowed S4-L0 from 37.4 to 23.4 tok/s, with a ~27 ms stall on 18 of 47 tokens in both modes, so event timings compare kernels but don't give absolute token time. Open: a host-only timeline (no events) to see what the unprofiled run overlaps | fork's `GGML_CUDA_PROFILE_HOST` (host time per graph evaluation, GPU time from two events per evaluation, no synchronization), S4-L0 with the kernels on and off |
| Q50 | Flint's shared-expert gate/up and its second dense layer reach the CUDA backend unfused (two products and a separate SWIGLU), because the fusion matcher wants gate, up and GLU as adjacent graph nodes. Fusing them saves 47 launches, activation quantizations and SWIGLU kernels per token | done | **+0.7 %, exact.** The shared expert's gate and up products now fuse with SWIGLU (13 instead of 16 kernels per MoE layer): not a graph-order problem, the fusion memory check refused them because the allocator reused the FFN input's buffer for the SWIGLU output ([note](../docs/research/notes/shared_expert_launches.md), fork branch `pascal-p106-shexp`). UD-Q4_K_XL on 6 cards, warmed llama-bench: tg128 33.99 → 34.24 tok/s, pp512 194.8 → 196.3. | Q51 | How many experts do consecutive Flint tokens share? Distinct experts per layer for 2, 4, 8 and 16 consecutive generated tokens against random routing (11.9 / 23.2 / 44.2 / 80.8). Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B shares about half between neighbours | done | **Little locality.** 2, 4, 8 and 16 consecutive generated tokens touch 11.1 / 20.1 / 35.6 / 59.7 distinct experts per layer (80 % of random at 8 tokens, against 51 % for Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B); adjacent tokens share 0.95 of 6. Whole-file edits route no differently (36.1 at 8). Layers 17–21 share the most (30–32 at 8 tokens), layers 2–6 the least (40–42). Renormalized route weights by rank 0.31 / 0.21 / 0.16 / 0.13 / 0.11 / 0.10; the sixth is below 5 % on only 6.4 % of token-layers, so skipping light experts gains nothing. Expert use is spread out: a layer's top 25 % of experts take 44 % of reads. So Q53's long block stays at 7 tokens, and a deduplicating multi-token `MUL_MAT_ID` kernel would save only 8–26 % of a 2–8-token verify's expert bytes | quant machine: Flint Q8_0 (sigmoid router, top-6 renormalized), 38 prompts (chat, code, whole-file edits, agent turns) generated at the makers' sampling (T 1.0) with exact token ids, 65,558 generated tokens; router top-6 and scores dumped for every position and layer ([bench/expert_overlap](../bench/expert_overlap/), [data](../docs/data/2026-10-23_q51/README.md)) |

Layer 1: cherry-pick upstream PR #31004 (graph key = first node plus its shape; keep the compute-6.1 gate and the fork's profiler key). Layer 2, only if rebuilds cost ≥ 2 %: per-shape graph results and schedulers in llama-context. Measure with A/B runs of `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` and `LLAMA_GRAPH_REUSE_DISABLE=1` and `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_DEBUG` reset counts. Worth est. +2–6 % with Q53's gate, +8–31 % for ungated drafters ([note](../docs/research/notes/speculation_plumbing.md)) |

The server logs "backend sampling is not compatible with grammar, disabling" for every tools request. +3.6–4.8 ms per token, slightly above the 3.2–3.5 ms estimate; short replies (150 tokens) are too noisy to read. Decides Q67: worth +12–16 % on agent traffic | rig 1 h: one `-bs` server, the same tools request with `tool_choice` none, none plus `backend_sampling: false`, and auto; T 0 and T 1.0 with seeds. Candidates with levels in registers decoded by byte permutes: est. 3.5–4 ps; IQ Pascal kernels with shared-memory tables (`pascal-p106-v3-iqmmvq`) measured 6.4–9.2 after two rounds of tuning (gate/up 6.67 IQ2_S, down 7.07 IQ3_XXS), so N2P/N3P est. ~1.8× faster per expert weight. **Proxy** (importance-weighted reconstruction error, 84 matrices of 7 layers): register-table levels match the IQ codebooks at equal size; ik_llama.cpp's shifted-table bit makes N2+ (2.6875 bits) / N3+ (3.25) a mix with 6 % less error than the best IQ mix of its size (2.875 bits); uniform levels and Q2_K/Q3_K +11–24 %, Hadamard rotation +9 %, mainline Q2_0 17× IQ3_XXS. Every idea from [flash_next_variants.md](../docs/research/notes/flash_next_variants.md) and
[sub_1_bit_quants.md](../docs/research/notes/sub_1_bit_quants.md) has a row here, including the ones the evidence argues
against (marked parked, with the reason and what would reopen them).

| ID | Question | Status | Result | How |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q213 | Do Flash-Next's rig-sized files fit nine P106 cards, and at what context? `--fit` projection of the Swift-1.5 GSQ-RCO IQ3_XXS (47.15 GB on the cards) and Q2_0 (37.74 GB) at 64k, 128k and 262k, q8_0 and F16 cache, `-ub` 128–512, with and without an MTP draft, `-ts` split so that each card holds at most one attention layer. The probe stops before the weights load, so sparse copies should do: the first 64 MiB of each shard relayed, each shard `truncate -s` to its full size (untested; otherwise relays of ~76 and ~67 GB) | done | **Q2_0 fits with room; IQ3_XXS only to 48k on whole layers.** 184 projections on header-only copies (12 MiB per first shard, 1 MiB per second, 55 MB in all), each row's split from `scripts/split_plan.py`, F16 cache, ≥ 150 MiB left on every card: Q2_0 262k at `-ub 64` (224 MiB), 128k at `-ub 128` and `256` (925, 664), 64k at `-ub 512` (735); with the MTP draft 128k at `-ub` ≤ 256 (309–678), 64k at `-ub 512` (519). IQ3_XXS 48k at `-ub 64` and `128` (247, 187), 32k at `-ub 256`, with the draft 32k at `-ub 64`; its layers run 701–1,214 MiB, and moving 5–10 expert tensors to the next card (`-ot`) reaches 128k (300 MiB) but turns off pipeline parallelism (Q509, Q510). 12 attention layers on 9 cards leave three cards with two, so the splits balance memory instead. q8_0 cache buys no context: the compute buffers grow by what the cache saves (Q508). Compute grows 13–16 B per micro-batch × context token (1,961 MiB per card at 262k, `-ub 512`), and limits 262k. The draft takes 2,243–4,462 MiB of the last card and the recurrent state ×4. Stock `d77256999` projects the same within 4 MiB. Q2_0 needs ≥ 7 cards at 64k, 8 at 128k; IQ3_XXS all nine. For R13: `-c 65536 -b 2048 -ub 512 -ts 6,5,5,6,5,5,6,5,6 -fit off` (735 MiB) | rig; [data](../docs/data/2026-11-03_flash_next_fit/README.md); [scripts/fit_probe.sh](../scripts/fit_probe.sh), [scripts/split_plan.py](../scripts/split_plan.py); sub_1_bit_quants.md E1 |

It needs no tensor cores: Strata's SIMT `block_scores_kernel`, `block_topk_kernel`, `attn_chunk_kernel_pre75` and `attn_merge_kernel` ran on a P40 (28+ SMs only so far), so this starts as a port; design on ids (Q441), and add the long-context select | written and built 2026-11-04, CPU-checked, untimed (the long-context select not written: ~0 on the agent check) | **Fork `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-qsa` `bdb902d95` (3 on `-ladder` `98bbd6a2f`), behind `GGML_CUDA_FATTN_SPARSE_PASCAL=1` (off by default); not yet run on any GPU.** `fattn-sparse-pascal.cu` takes FLASH_ATTN_EXT calls that carry the `n_kv_max` hint on GPUs without Turing tensor cores (head 128 or 256, 4–16 query heads per KV head, F16 or Q8_0, at most 8 query tokens): upstream's SIMT compaction lists each query token's kept cells, then one gather kernel splits each list over ~4 blocks per SM (one warp per 4 query heads of a KV head, each K/V row converted once per warp, scores of 8 keys × 4 heads per transpose-reduce, online softmax in base 2, the last block of a column merges the splits and adds the sinks): two launches per layer, 113–161 registers, no stack. The fork's own design (its `fattn-pascal.cu` layout, upstream's compaction); no Strata code. CPU check of the kernel's host model against FP64 and ggml's CPU kernel: 20 / 20 cases (10 shapes × F16/Q8_0), error ≤ 9.5·10⁻⁷ of the largest output; the 3 fault codes and 4 source mutations each fail. Est. −3 to −5 ms a token at 5k cells, −15 to −18 at 16k, −34 to −37 at 32k, −145 to −150 at 128k (F16); agent check est. +3–6 % generation. Mode 1 takes a call from 1.5 × 2,051 cells per query token (verify batches: Q643). With it on, C152/Q702's dense-kernel gain applies below that threshold and to prompts. The rig's DFlash2 and MTP lines do not replay (drafts within `n_rs_seq`); draft-simple and n-gram drafters on hybrids, and targets that cannot remove part of a sequence, do; n-gram drafts carry no distributions and draw nothing from the RNG (by reading), so the bias needs a drafter with probabilities. | Q875 | (C315, from Q781's PTX run) llama.cpp's MMQ reads out of bounds when a token's expert ids repeat: the fork's `test_mul_mat_id_dup` cases (`dup=1`) of the grid types at 5–8 tokens, with the Pascal kernels skipped (`GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_PASCAL_SKIP`, or `_MTOK_SKIP` for 5–8 tokens), go to `ggml_cuda_mul_mat_q`, whose `quantize_mmq_q8_1` (D4 layout) then reads 16-byte chunks up to ~152 KB past its 7.6 MB input. Seen on an RTX 3090 through the rig build's compute 6.1 PTX: under compute-sanitizer every IQ2_S case of 5–8 tokens is wrong (error 1.6–2.3) and the run ends in an MMQ launch failure; a plain run ends in an illegal-address fault. `-ladder3`'s multi-token kernels pass the same cases (0 memcheck errors). This is upstream's known limitation, not a new bug: upstream keeps its own repeated-id cases disabled for CUDA (`TAG_MUL_MAT_ID_DUPLICATE_ID`), and routers never repeat an expert within a token, so no model reaches it and no upstream report is needed; the PTX runs' stock arms leave these cases out. TQ2_T is 128 weights in 34 bytes: an FP16 scale and one byte per step of 4 weights, a 16-bit tail-biting state whose low 15 bits one 32-bit multiply hashes into two 11-bit indices of a fixed 2,048-point 2-D codebook. agentionai's llama.cpp fork (`9aa51ce4f`) has CUDA kernels for it, so no microbench of our own was needed: on CUDA the codebook is int8 (4 KB in shared memory) and a step is one multiply, two 2-byte reads and one `dp4a`; one token `mul_mat_vec_q`, 2–8 tokens the MoE kernel, 9 and more MMQ with Pascal `dp4a` configurations. The Gyro files also rotate every routed expert's input at run time (`prism.hadamard`: signs and a 128-wide FWHT before gate/up and before down, 144 tensors) | done 2026-11-04 (C335r, rig 1) | **On a P106 (C335r, [gyro_p106](../docs/data/2026-11-04_gyro_p106/README.md)): TQ2_T misses the ~5 ps line at every token count**: routed gate/up 23.8 ps per weight at one token (10.0 with its one-row layout), 8.4–10.5 at 2–8 tokens; routed down 37.8 (22.3 with `wide0`), 15.2–19.2 at 2–8; Miner Llama's Q2_0 on the same card 3.3 / 3.9 ps at one token, 2.8–3.6 / 5.4–9.6 at 2–8 (3–10× faster per weight read). Correct on the card (81/81, 53/53, 38/38). Est. ~+20 ms a token at one token for Gyro-M's agent KLD 0.148 against Swift Q2_0's 0.285: the trade that decides Q908. The fork builds for compute 6.1 as it is (no tensor cores or `cp.async`; AVX2 only behind a run-time check); rig tarball md5 `97bc088b…` (BUILDS.md) with two patches of ours: P106-sized perf cases (TQ2_T and Q2_0 experts at 1–8 tokens, Q8_0 dense, the rotation) and `GGML_CUDA_TQ_MMVQ_ROWS=1` before Turing (Q925). **Correct through the 6.1 PTX on an RTX 3090:** `MUL_MAT` 486/486 for all six trellis types; TQ2_T `MUL_MAT_ID` 111/111, `GET_ROWS` 8/8; the one-token cases in the layout a P106 takes (`GGML_CUDA_TQ_MMVQ_ROWS=0` on the 3090) 23/23 and 15/15. The fork picks its one-token multi-row layout for long trellis rows only from Turing on, so on a P106 a 2,560-wide gate/up row runs one per 128-thread block (two passes over 20 blocks, the second three-quarters idle). The P106 number (ps per weight and GB/s against Miner Llama's Q2_0 kernel and the ~5 ps line, the rotation's cost per token): C335r on rig 1. Cost files for `LLAMA_SPEC_ADAPT_COST` in the folder. **Dense products (C70, 2026-11-04; not yet run on a GPU): k-column kernels `mmvq-pascal-kcol*.cu`, fork `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-kcol` `e1fa4c573` (on the ladder branch), for Q8_0, Q2_0, Q3_K, Q4_K, Q5_K, Q6_K and IQ4_XS `MUL_MAT` of 1–8 tokens on compute 6.1, and the BF16 kernel (Q546) to 8 tokens.** Each weight is read and decoded once into int8 words and multiplied with dp4a by every column; the activations are quantized from FP32 (as `quantize_q8_1`) into records per lane unit and column (int8 values in each type's decode order, FP32 scales, the K-quants' block sums, Q3_K's/Q6_K's half-block offsets as the dp4a chains' initial values, bank-conflict padding) that each block stages in shared memory once: per 32 weights, row and column 8 dp4a and 3–5 FP32 operations. Weights staged per lane group (16-byte segments) or per warp from each row's 16-byte boundary (2-byte-aligned blocks, short rows). A column's sum depends on the row width only, the same for every column count. BF16: lanes write several outputs, so 4 rows per lane group serve 3–4 tokens (2 before) and 8 rows serve 5–8. Default on for 2–8 columns, plus one column for Q3_K (Q224) and Q8_0's short rows (Q604); `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_PASCAL_KCOL=0|1|2` and launch-shape overrides. 56–244 registers, no spills; each type's decoding matches ggml's reference dequantization on a CPU (`bench/mmvq_pascal/verify/kcol_unit_check`, 7/7 types, 240 cases each; a mutation fails exactly the types it touches). Est. 5-column cost against one column at memory speed: Q8_0, Q4_K–Q6_K ~1.0×, IQ4_XS ~1.1×, Q3_K ~1.4× (decode-bound past ~3 columns), BF16 ~1.0–1.2×. `bench/mmvq_pascal` `build/mmvq_kcol` (Flash-Next's dense shapes, per-pass totals), test-backend-ops cases at 1–8 tokens; the P106 run next (R16). New Q629–Q632. [data](../docs/data/2026-11-04_kcol_dense/README.md) **Routed experts (R7, 2026-11-04; not yet run on a GPU): `mul_mat_vec_q2_0_pascal_mtok`, fork `pascal-p106-v3-b12076-mtok` `c70687382` (on the ladder branch), takes every Q2_0 `MUL_MAT_ID` of 2–8 tokens on compute 6.1.** The entries that picked one expert form a group (found per warp with 8 ballots over the ids); its block reads the expert's rows once, looks each code word up once and multiplies it by every member, with the members' activations staged in shared memory once per block and 4 row blocks per block; lane groups, staging and sums are the one-token kernel's, so the results are bit-identical to k one-token passes. Fused gate/up + GLU, biases and the routed + shared launch as before; `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_PASCAL_MTOK=E,CAP,RB,G` turns it off or selects the group size, row blocks and gate loads. 76–168 registers, no spills. The template renders the replies' text exactly (159,363 tokens against 159,598 generated; tool calls byte-exact). Data run 1's exact ids: 76 of 1,247 turns (6.1 %; 45 % of those over 4k tokens) change ids in a text round trip (`file` + `type` where the tokenizer writes `filetype`), 65 of them ≥ 256 tokens before the end, beyond the live ring at `-ub 128`. **Fix:** `LLAMA_SERVER_TEXT_REUSE=1` keeps the cached ids past the first difference for as long as both spell the same bytes (cut where a token ends in both), so the text is unchanged and the model reads its own ids. CPU check (tiny Flint, `-fa off`): 10 of 10 trials reuse prompt + reply (without it: the 18-token checkpoint and 744–1,177 tokens processed), answers equal to a cold server's on the same ids ([data](../docs/data/2026-10-28_reply_rereads/README.md)) | for the v3 release line, with Q263 (`pascal-p106-v3-promptpath` `8130a649d`); Q364 offers it upstream |

### Elixir fine-tuning

Every idea from the 2026-11-02 fine-tuning research has a row here, including the ones the evidence argues against
(marked parked, with the reason and what would reopen them). Score is hidden `mix test` passing with tests unchanged, in
three arms: base, tuned, opponent. The claim under test: on Elixir repositories it never saw, a tuned model solves more
tasks per rig-hour than Flint and than its own base with a good context file. All times are estimates.

### Agentic RL and a DeepSeek teacher

Every idea from the 2026-11-03 research on FireFlash's agentic RL recipe and DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017 as a teacher has a
row here ([report](https://github.com/Cinderella-Man/llm-finetunning/blob/main/reports/Agentic%20RL%20for%20a%20DeepSeek%20teacher.md)).
The verdict: use DeepSeek as it is, through its own API (MIT weights; its terms allow distillation), as a test-filtered
data source; put RL on the student; train the teacher only for an Elixir tail it cannot reach with 50 attempts and hints.
Home generation with dsx is est. 1.2–2.4 kept sessions per hour, which revises Q501's 5–10. Costs are estimates from list
prices.

| ID | Question | Status | Result | How |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q524 | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017 on Elixir through DeepSeek's own API, off-peak: pass@1 and pass@8 on 50 mined tasks, and three seeds on the Elixir SWE Bench under pi. DeepSeek writes the data only if it beats the best local teacher (Flash-Next 10–11 of 20) on kept sessions per dollar and on coverage at k = 8 | planned | — | API, est. $10–35 plus bench runs; the owner's OK for the spend and for DeepSeek as a writer; Q478/Q479's tasks |
| Q525 | Teacher harness and effort on Q524's tasks: pi against a minimal harness (DeepSeek's own table: DeepSWE v1.1 66.2 in pi, 74.2 in mini-SWE), effort 70 against 100 (25 → 100 lifts it 66.0 → 74.2 at ~2.5× the output). Sessions stay in the student's harness unless the gap is large, since a student learns the protocol it was recorded in | planned | — | API, with Q524 |
| Q526 | FireFlash's nine grader gates as CI for every mined Elixir task: the reference fix passes in the training sandbox, every checked requirement is stated, offline, reliable boot, no leaked answer, no free credit, any correct solution passes, malformed output is scored, scoring is deterministic (fixed `mix test` seed, repeated runs for timing-dependent tests). Plus an offline Hex cache that cannot hold the target's later releases, and a single-commit repository | planned | — | code, in `gate.py tasks` |
| Q527 | Audit the tasks no attempt ever solves before sampling them more: FireFlash found nearly half of its never-solved tasks broken rather than hard | planned | — | after Q524 and Q528's first pass |

Est. $150–580 per 2,000 kept sessions at a 30 % pass rate (dsx at home: 35–72 days). Stop the tail when a newly covered task costs more than ~$5 | planned | — | API; after Q524, Q526, the owner's writer list, and DeepSeek's rate limits read |
